============================================================================\n\nval_t rayTriangleIntersect(const Vec3& orig, const Vec3& dir,\n                            const Vec3& v0, const Vec3& ============================================================================\n\n// Check if a ray between two triangles is blocked by any other triangle\n// Uses octree for O(log N) instead of O(N) Skipping costs ~0.3 ns\n// per draw versus ~10 us per pair of real work, so the blocks are sized so\n// that (skip cost + compute cost) is balanced across threads: early blocks\n// (cheap skips) get more rows than late ones.\nvoid computeFormFactors(SimulationState& state) {\n    printf(\"Computing form factors (Kij)...\\\\n\");\n\n    const size_t N = state.numTriangles;\n    const std::vector<Triangle>& tris = state.triangles;\n\n    // --- Pass 1: random draws consumed per row and stream offsets ----------\n    std::vector<unsigned long long> rowPairs(N); i < N && samples < 64; i += std::max<size_t>(1, N / 8)) {\n            for (size_t j = 0; j < N && samples < 64; j += std::max<size_t>(1, N / 8)) {\n                if (i == j || sameFacing(tris[i], tris[j])) continue;\n                sink = sink + computeKij(i, j, tris, state.octree, probe);\n                ++samples;\n            }\n        }\n        t1 = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();\n        if (samples > 0) {\n            pairNs = std::chrono::duration<double, std::nano>(t1 - t0).count() / double(samples);\n        }\n        (void)sink;\n    }\n    const double rho = skipNsPerDraw / std::max(pairNs, 1.0);   // pair-equivalents per draw\n\n    // --- Pass 3: balanced contiguous row blocks ---------------------------\n    const int maxThreads = omp_get_max_threads();\n    const int P = static_cast<int>(std::min<size_t>(static_cast<size_t>(std::max(maxThreads, 1)), std::max<size_t>(N, 1)));\n    std::vector<size_t> blockStart(P + 1, N);\n    {\n        // For a given per-thread budget T, assign rows greedily; feasible if all rows fit.\n        auto assign = [&](double budget, std::vector<size_t>* out) -> bool {\n            size_t row = 0;\n            for (int k = 0; k < P; ++k) {\n                if (out) (*out)[k] = row;\n                double acc = double(rowOffset[row]) * rho;\n                bool first = true;\n                while (row < N && (first || acc + double(rowPairs[row]) <= budget)) {\n                    acc += double(rowPairs[row]);\n                    ++row;\n                    first = false;\n                }\n            }\n            if (out) (*out)[P] = N;\n            return row >= N;\n        };\n        double totalWork = 0.0;\n        for (size_t i = 0; i < N; ++i) totalWork += double(rowPairs[i]);\n        double lo = 0.0, hi = totalWork + double(rowOffset[N]) * rho + 1.0;\n        for (int it = 0; it < 64; ++it) {\n            double mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi);\n            if (assign(mid, nullptr)) hi = mid; else lo = mid;\n        }\n        assign(hi, &blockStart);\n    }\n\n    // --- Pass 4: compute --------------------------------------------------\n    size_t progress = 0;   // rows completed (atomic updates)\n    #pragma omp parallel num_threads(P)\n    {\n        const int k = omp_get_thread_num();\n        const size_t rBegin = blockStart[k];\n        const size_t rEnd = blockStart[k + 1];\n        if (rBegin < rEnd) {\n            RandomGenerator rng(42);\n            rng.discard(rowOffset[rBegin]);\n            for (size_t i = rBegin; The same static partition is used for every\n    // timestep so each thread keeps revisiting its own rows of kij/tau.\n    #pragma omp parallel\n    {\n        for (size_t t = 0; t < T; ++t) {\n            #pragma omp for schedule(static)\n            for (size_t i = 0; Each thread\n// needs the engine state at its own offset in that stream. std::mt19937::\n// discard is O(n); for large offsets we instead jump in O(log n) polynomial\n// applications over GF(2) (Haramoto et al., \"Efficient Jump Ahead for\n// F2-Linear Random Number Generators\"): the state transition A satisfies\n// phi(A) = 0 for the characteristic polynomial phi (degree 19937), so\n// A^d = (x^d mod phi)(A). The engine state is moved in and out of the\n// std::mt19937 object through its stream operators (libstdc++ layout:\n// 624 words followed by the index). Everything here is verified against\n// std::mt19937::discard at runtime before being used; on any mismatch the\n// code falls back to the library discard, so results never depend on it.\nnamespace mtjump {\n\nconstexpr int N = 624;\nconstexpr int M = 397;\nconstexpr uint32_t UM = 0x80000000u;\nconstexpr uint32_t LM = 0x7fffffffu;\nconstexpr uint32_t MA = 0x9908b0dfu;\nconstexpr int DEG = 19937;                 // degree of the characteristic polynomial\nconstexpr int PW = DEG / 64 + 1;           // 64-bit words per polynomial (312)\n\nusing Poly = std::vector<uint64_t>;\n\n// One full regeneration of the 624-word state array (same recurrence as\n// libstdc++'s _M_gen_rand, written as three loops so it vectorizes).\ninline void regen(uint32_t* __restrict x) {\n    for (int i = 0;   // index of the next word to output (0..N)\n};\n\ninline bool extract(const std::mt19937& e, State& s) {\n    std::stringstream ss;\n    ss << e;\n    for (int i = 0; i < N; ++i) {\n        unsigned long long v;\n        if (!(ss >> v) || v > 0xffffffffULL) return false;\n        s.x[i] = static_cast<uint32_t>(v);\n    }\n    if (!(ss >> s.p) || s.p > static_cast<unsigned long long>(N)) return false;\n    std::string extra;\n    if (ss >> extra) return false;   // unexpected layout\n    return true;\n}\n\ninline bool inject(std::mt19937& e, const State& s) {\n    std::stringstream ss;\n    for (int i = 0; i < N; ++i) ss << s.x[i] << ' ';\n    ss << s.p;\n    ss >> e;\n    return !ss.fail();\n}\n\ninline bool getBit(const Poly& a, size_t i) { return (a[i >> 6] >> (i & 63)) & 1u; }\ninline void setBit(Poly& a, size_t i) { a[i >> 6] |= uint64_t(1) << (i & 63); }\n\n// a ^= b << s   (a must be large enough)\ninline void xorShifted(Poly& a, const Poly& b, size_t s) {\n    size_t q = s >> 6, r = s & 63;\n    size_t nb = b.size();\n    if (r == 0) {\n        for (size_t i = 0; (any nonzero linear functional of the state works\n// because the characteristic polynomial is irreducible).\ninline bool characteristicPolynomial(Poly& phi) {\n    const size_t NB = 2 * static_cast<size_t>(DEG);\n    State s;\n    if (!extract(std::mt19937(42), s)) return false;\n    // Sequence words X_0 .. idx < static_cast<size_t>(N) && k < NB; ++idx) seq[k++] = x[idx] & 1u;\n            regen(x);\n            idx = 0;\n        }\n    }\n    // Reversed, bit-packed sequence: R bit j = seq[NB-1-j]; padded by 64 bits.\n    const size_t RW = NB / 64 + 3;\n    Poly R(RW, 0);\n    for (size_t j = 0; The low 31 bits of word 0 are \"dead\" state\n// (never used by the recurrence) and may end up wrong; callers always finish\n// with a full regen(), which only reads the top bit of word 0.\ninline void applyPoly(const Poly& g, uint32_t* x, std::vector<uint32_t>& buf) {\n    int deg = DEG - 1;\n    while (deg > 0 && !getBit(g, static_cast<size_t>(deg))) --deg;\n    buf.assign(static_cast<size_t>(N + deg + 1), 0u);\n    uint32_t* b = buf.data();\n    size_t t = 0;\n    for (int i = deg; i >= 0; --i) {\n        uint32_t y = (b[t] & UM) | (b[t + 1] & LM);\n        b[t + N] = b[t + M] ^ (y >> 1) ^ ((0u - (y & 1u)) & MA);\n        ++t;\n        if (getBit(g, static_cast<size_t>(i))) {\n            uint32_t* __restrict w = b + t;\n            for (int j = 0; j < N; ++j) w[j] ^= x[j];\n        }\n    }\n    std::memcpy(x, b + t, sizeof(uint32_t) * N);\n}\n\n// Advance engine e by d outputs. Uses the jump table for the bulk of the\n// skip when it is available and the skip is long; otherwise regenerates\n// block by block. Returns false (engine untouched) if the state layout is\n// not understood.\ninline bool skip(std::mt19937& e, unsigned long long d, const JumpTable* jt,\n                 unsigned long long minJumpWords, std::vector<uint32_t>& scratch) {\n    State s;\n    if (!extract(e, s)) return false;\n    unsigned long long q = s.p + d;\n    unsigned long long blocks = q / N;\n    if (blocks == 0) {\n        s.p = q;\n    } else {\n        unsigned long long words = (blocks - 1) * N;\n        if (jt && jt->ok && words >= minJumpWords) {\n            for (size_t j = 0; they\n    // are only enabled when the stream is long enough for them to pay off.\n    using Clock = std::chrono::steady_clock;\n    auto ms = [](Clock::time_point a, Clock::time_point b) {\n        return std::chrono::duration<double, std::milli>(b - a).count();\n    };\n\n    double skipNsPerDraw = 0.35;        // std::mt19937::discard, typical\n    bool fastSkip = false;\n    if (double(totalDraws) * skipNsPerDraw > 10.0 * 1e6) {\n        fastSkip = mtjump::selfTest(nullptr, ~0ULL);\n        std::vector<uint32_t> scratch;\n        std::mt19937 probe(42);\n        auto t0 = Clock::now();\n        if (fastSkip) mtjump::skip(probe, 1ULL << 20, nullptr, ~0ULL, scratch);\n        else probe.discard(1ULL << 20);\n        auto t1 = Clock::now();\n        skipNsPerDraw = ms(t0, t1) * 1e6 / double(1ULL << 20);\n    }\n\n    // Polynomial jump: fixed cost is ~45 ms (table + self-test) plus ~10 ms\n    // per thread; the expected saving is about half of the longest\n    // regeneration-based skip (threads skip concurrently, which also slows\n    // the regeneration itself down).\n    mtjump::JumpTable jumpTable;\n    double jumpNs = 0.0;                // per-thread cost of a long jump\n    bool useJump = false;\n    if (fastSkip && double(totalDraws) * skipNsPerDraw > 200.0 * 1e6) {\n        int bits = 1;\n        while ((totalDraws >> bits) != 0) ++bits;\n        if (mtjump::buildTable(jumpTable, bits + 1)) {\n            std::vector<uint32_t> scratch;\n            std::mt19937 probe(42);\n            auto t0 = Clock::now();\n            bool ok = mtjump::skip(probe, totalDraws, &jumpTable, 0, scratch);\n            auto t1 = Clock::now();\n            jumpNs = ms(t0, t1) * 1e6;\n            useJump = ok && mtjump::selfTest(&jumpTable, 0);\n        }\n    }\n    // Draws below this length are skipped by regeneration instead of jumping.\n    const unsigned long long minJumpWords =\n        useJump ? static_cast<unsigned long long>(jumpNs / std::max(skipNsPerDraw, 1e-3)) : ~0ULL;\n\n    // Time a few real pairs with a scratch generator (results discarded) to\n    // relate skip cost to compute cost. Only needed when skips are not free.\n    double pairNs = 15000.0;\n    if (double(totalDraws) * skipNsPerDraw > 2.0 * 1e6) {\n        RandomGenerator probe(42);\n        volatile val_t sink = ZERO;\n        size_t samples = 0;\n        auto t0 = Clock::now();\n        for (size_t i = 0; i < N && samples < 32; i += std::max<size_t>(1, N / 8)) {\n            for (size_t j = 0; j < N && samples < 32; j += std::max<size_t>(1, N / 8)) {\n                if (i == j || sameFacing(tris[i], tris[j])) continue;\n                sink = sink + computeKij(i, j, tris, state.octree, probe);\n                ++samples;\n            }\n        }\n        auto t1 = Clock::now();\n        if (samples > 0) pairNs = ms(t0, t1) * 1e6 / double(samples);\n        (void)sink;\n    }\n    // Cost (in pair-equivalents) of positioning a generator at stream offset o.\n    auto skipCost = [&](unsigned long long o) {\n        double c = double(o) * skipNsPerDraw;\n        if (useJump) c = std::min(c, jumpNs);\n        return c / std::max(pairNs, 1.0);\n    };\n\n    // --- Pass 3: balanced contiguous pair ranges --------------------------\n    // Work is partitioned at pair granularity in row-major order. Thread k\n    // handles the non-culled pairs with ordinals [pairBegin[k], pairBegin[k+1])\n    // (plus the culled/diagonal positions in between, which are free).\n    const int maxThreads = omp_get_max_threads();\n    const int P = static_cast<int>(std::min<unsigned long long>(\n        static_cast<unsigned long long>(std::max(maxThreads, 1)), std::max<unsigned long long>(totalPairs, 1)));\n    std::vector<unsigned long long> pairBegin(P + 1, totalPairs);\n    {\n        auto assign = [&](double budget, std::vector<unsigned long long>* out) -> bool {\n            unsigned long long a = 0;\n            for (int k = 0; k < P; ++k) {\n                if (out) (*out)[k] = a;\n                double room = budget - skipCost(a * DRAWS_PER_PAIR);\n                unsigned long long take = 1;\n                if (room > 1.0) take = static_cast<unsigned long long>(std::min(room, double(totalPairs)));\n                a = std::min(totalPairs, a + take);\n            }\n            if (out) (*out)[P] = totalPairs;\n            return a >= totalPairs;\n        };\n        double lo = 0.0, hi = double(totalPairs) + skipCost(totalDraws) + 1.0;\n        for (int it = 0; it < 64; ++it) {\n            double mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi);\n            if (assign(mid, nullptr)) hi = mid; else lo = mid;\n        }\n        assign(hi, &pairBegin);\n    }\n\n    // Row-major position (i*N + j) of the non-culled pair with ordinal `ord`.\n    auto positionOf = [&](unsigned long long ord) -> size_t {\n        if (ord >= totalPairs) return N * N;\n        size_t i = static_cast<size_t>(std::upper_bound(pairPrefix.begin(), pairPrefix.end(), ord) - pairPrefix.begin()) - 1;\n        unsigned long long remaining = ord - pairPrefix[i];\n        for (size_t j = 0; {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-fable-5-1","id":"msg_011Cg6V53fUZVd5nwawxAeF4","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Done. The roomsim benchmark is now OpenMP-parallel with bit-identical results to the original. Only `roomsim/roomsim.cpp` and `roomsim/CMakeLists.txt` changed, the executable is still `roomsim`, and OpenMP is required unconditionally via `find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)`.\n\n**Results on this 128-core / 256-thread machine** (default args, hashes identical to the original at every size):\n\n| Triangles | Original total | Parallel total (256 threads) |\n|---|---|---|\n| 320 (default) | 1485 ms | 14 ms |\n| 1280 | 26.5 s | 235 ms |\n| 5120 | 469 s | 3.3 s |\n| 20480 | not run sequentially | 79 s |\n\nScaling at 1280 triangles is near-linear through 128 threads (26.3 s, 13.1 s, 6.6 s, 3.3 s, 1.7 s, 0.85 s, 0.44 s, 0.33 s for 1 to 128 threads). The output hash is identical for every thread count tested, including 1, 2, 3, 200 and 256, plus edge cases such as negative reflectivity, zero timesteps, out-of-range source index and the 20-triangle mesh.\n\n**What was done**\n\n- **Form factors (99% of runtime):** The original draws all Monte Carlo samples from one sequential Mersenne Twister in row-major pair order. To keep results bit-exact, a cheap counting pass determines how many draws each row consumes (64 per non-culled pair, none per culled pair). Work is then split into contiguous pair ranges, and each thread positions a private generator at its stream offset. For long offsets this uses an exact MT19937 jump-ahead (characteristic polynomial via Berlekamp-Massey, powers of x modulo it applied to the state), which turns a 600 ms skip into 7 ms. The jump code is self-tested against the library discard at startup and falls back to it on any mismatch, so correctness never depends on it. Ranges are balanced to account for skip cost.\n- **Time delays, simulation, distances, validation:** parallelized over rows with static scheduling. The simulation processes blocks of timesteps closer than the minimum delay with one barrier per block, and keeps the inner accumulation order unchanged.\n- **Large matrices** are first-touched by the threads that fill them instead of being zero-filled serially.\n- **Thread binding:** when the runtime hasn't been told to bind and more than half the CPUs are in use, threads are pinned to distinct CPUs. Without this, unbound threads above 128 lost about 10 ms per region start to scheduler collisions, which dominated the default size.\n\n**Things to be aware of**\n\n- All parallel regions use the same team size on purpose. Shrinking the team for one phase made libgomp take seconds to re-form the full team afterwards.\n- The 20480-triangle case was not compared to the original (that run would take over two hours sequentially), but it validates and gives identical hashes at 200 and 256 threads.\n- The machine is shared; one timing outlier during testing coincided with a load-average spike from another user's job."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":32,"cache_creation_input_tokens":577,"cache_read_input_tokens":159971,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":577},"output_tokens":783,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global","speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ed33e910-89a5-48bb-941f-245fcc855aa9","uuid":"258fd756-7208-476e-9c07-609da9e5c263","timestamp":"2026-10-16T19:41:11.531Z","request_id":"req_011Cg6V53KspxByMj4FHBbTK"}

The roomsim benchmark is now OpenMP-parallel with bit-identical results to the original. Only `roomsim/roomsim.cpp` and `roomsim/CMakeLists.txt` changed, the executable is still `roomsim`, and OpenMP is required unconditionally via `find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)`.\n\n**Results on this 128-core / 256-thread machine** (default args, hashes identical to the original at every size):\n\n| Triangles | Original total | Parallel total (256 threads) |\n|---|---|---|\n| 320 (default) | 1485 ms | 14 ms |\n| 1280 | 26.5 s | 235 ms |\n| 5120 | 469 s | 3.3 s |\n| 20480 | not run sequentially | 79 s |\n\nScaling at 1280 triangles is near-linear through 128 threads (26.3 s, 13.1 s, 6.6 s, 3.3 s, 1.7 s, 0.85 s, 0.44 s, 0.33 s for 1 to 128 threads). The output hash is identical for every thread count tested, including 1, 2, 3, 200 and 256, plus edge cases such as negative reflectivity, zero timesteps, out-of-range source index and the 20-triangle mesh.\n\n**What was done**\n\n- **Form factors (99% of runtime):** The original draws all Monte Carlo samples from one sequential Mersenne Twister in row-major pair order. To keep results bit-exact, a cheap counting pass determines how many draws each row consumes (64 per non-culled pair, none per culled pair). Work is then split into contiguous pair ranges, and each thread positions a private generator at its stream offset. For long offsets this uses an exact MT19937 jump-ahead (characteristic polynomial via Berlekamp-Massey, powers of x modulo it applied to the state), which turns a 600 ms skip into 7 ms. The jump code is self-tested against the library discard at startup and falls back to it on any mismatch, so correctness never depends on it. Ranges are balanced to account for skip cost.\n- **Time delays, simulation, distances, validation:** parallelized over rows with static scheduling. The simulation processes blocks of timesteps closer than the minimum delay with one barrier per block, and keeps the inner accumulation order unchanged.\n- **Large matrices** are first-touched by the threads that fill them instead of being zero-filled serially.\n- **Thread binding:** when the runtime hasn't been told to bind and more than half the CPUs are in use, threads are pinned to distinct CPUs. Without this, unbound threads above 128 lost about 10 ms per region start to scheduler collisions, which dominated the default size.\n\n**Things to be aware of**\n\n- All parallel regions use the same team size on purpose.
